class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Gisela_of_Burgundy" title="Gisela of Burgundy">Gisela of Burgundy</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religion</th><td class="infobox-data"><a <p><b>Henry II</b> (<a href="/wiki/German_language" title="German language">German</a>: <i lang="de">Heinrich II</i>; <a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Enrico II</i>; 6 May 973 – 13 July 1024), also known as <b>Saint Henry the Exuberant</b>, <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Saint_Benedict" class="mw-redirect" title="Order of Saint Benedict">Obl. S. B.</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup> was <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Holy Roman Emperor">Holy Roman Emperor</a> ("Romanorum Imperator") from 1014. He died without an heir in 1024, and was  the last ruler of the <a href="/wiki/Ottonian_dynasty" title="Ottonian dynasty">Ottonian line</a>. As <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Bavaria" class="mw-redirect" title="Duke of Bavaria">Duke of Bavaria</a>, appointed in 995, Henry became <a href="/wiki/King_of_the_Romans" title="King of the Romans">King of the Romans</a> ("Rex Romanorum") following the sudden death of his second cousin, <a href="/wiki/Emperor_Otto_III" class="mw-redirect" title="Emperor Otto III">Emperor Otto III</a> in 1002, was made <a href="/wiki/King_of_Italy" title="King of Italy">King of Italy</a> ("Rex Italiae") in 1004, and crowned emperor by Pope Benedict VIII in 1014.
</p><p>The son of <a href="/wiki/Henry_II,_Duke_of_Bavaria" title="Henry II, Duke of Bavaria">Henry II, Duke of Bavaria</a>, and his wife <a href="/wiki/Gisela_of_Burgundy" title="Gisela of Burgundy">Gisela of Burgundy</a>, Emperor Henry II was a great-grandson of <a href="/wiki/German_king" class="mw-redirect" title="German king">German king</a> <a href="/wiki/Henry_the_Fowler" title="Henry the Fowler">Henry the Fowler</a> and a member of the Bavarian branch of the <a href="/wiki/Ottonian_dynasty" title="Ottonian dynasty">Ottonian dynasty</a>. Since his father had rebelled against two previous emperors, the younger Henry spent long periods of time in exile, where he turned to Christianity at an early age, first finding refuge with the <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Freising" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Freising">Bishop of Freising</a> and later during his education at the <a href="/wiki/Hildesheim_Cathedral" title="Hildesheim Cathedral">cathedral school</a> in Hildesheim. He succeeded his father as <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Bavaria" class="mw-redirect" title="Duke of Bavaria">Duke of Bavaria</a> in 995 as "Henry IV". As duke, he attempted to join his second-cousin, Emperor Otto III, in suppressing a revolt against imperial rule in Italy in 1002. Before Henry II could arrive, however, Otto III died of fever, leaving no heir. After defeating several contenders to the throne, Henry II was crowned <a href="/wiki/King_of_Germany" class="mw-redirect" title="King of Germany">King of Germany</a> on 9 July 1002 as the first in a line of kings to adopt the title <i>Rex Romanorum</i>, an allusion to his perceived prerogative to the future appointment of <i>Imperator Romanorum</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Germany1740_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Germany1740-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag failed verification of its source citation(s). (May 2020)">failed verification</span></a>&#32;– <a href="/wiki/Talk:Henry_II,_Holy_Roman_Emperor#Rex_Romanorum?" title="Talk:Henry II, Holy Roman Emperor">see discussion</a></i>&#93;</sup> On 15 May 1004, he was anointed <a href="/wiki/King_of_Italy" title="King of Italy">King of Italy</a> ("Rex Italiae"). In that same year, Henry II joined Duke <a href="/wiki/Jarom%C3%ADr_of_Bohemia" class="mw-redirect" title="Jaromír of Bohemia">Jaromír of Bohemia</a> in his struggle against the Poles, thus effectively incorporating the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Bohemia" title="Duchy of Bohemia">Duchy of Bohemia</a> into the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire" title="Holy Roman Empire">Holy Roman Empire</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Germany1740_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Germany1740-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Unlike his predecessor Otto III, who had imposed plans on sovereign administration and active political involvement in <a href="/wiki/Italy_in_the_Middle_Ages" title="Italy in the Middle Ages">Italy</a>, Henry spent most of his reign concerned with the <i>renovation</i> of the imperial territories north of the <a href="/wiki/Alps" title="Alps">Alps</a>, a policy summed up on his seal as <i>Renovatio regni Francorum</i>, which replaced Otto's <i>Renovatio imperii Romanorum</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Blumenthal1988_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Blumenthal1988-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> A <a href="/wiki/German-Polish_War_(1002%E2%80%931018)" class="mw-redirect" title="German-Polish War (1002–1018)">series of conflicts</a> with the Polish <a href="/wiki/Boles%C5%82aw_I_Chrobry" class="mw-redirect" title="Bolesław I Chrobry">Duke Bolesław I</a>, who had already conquered a number of countries surrounding him, required Henry II's full attention and years of political and military maneuvering. Henry did, however, lead three expeditions into Italy to enforce his feudal claim (<i>Honor Imperii</i>): twice to suppress secessionist revolts and once to address <a href="/wiki/Byzantine_Empire" title="Byzantine Empire">Byzantine</a> attempts to obtain dominance over <a href="/wiki/Southern_Italy" title="Southern Italy">southern Italy</a>. On 14 February 1014, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_VIII" title="Pope Benedict VIII">Pope Benedict VIII</a> crowned Henry <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Holy Roman Emperor">Holy Roman Emperor</a> in <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>.
</p><p>The rule of Henry II has been characterized as a period of centralized authority throughout the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire" title="Holy Roman Empire">Holy Roman Empire</a>. He consolidated his power by cultivating personal and political ties with the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a>. He greatly expanded the Ottonian dynasty's custom of employing clerics as counter-weights against secular nobles. Through donations to the Church and the establishment of new dioceses, Henry strengthened imperial rule across the Empire and increased control over ecclesiastical affairs. He stressed service to the Church and promoted monastic reform. For his remarkable personal piety and enthusiastic promotion of the Church, he was <a href="/wiki/Canonized" class="mw-redirect" title="Canonized">canonized</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Eugene_III" title="Pope Eugene III">Pope Eugene III</a> in 1146. He is the only medieval German monarch to ever have been honoured a <a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">saint</a>. Henry II's wife was the equally pious <a href="/wiki/Cunigunde_of_Luxembourg" title="Cunigunde of Luxembourg">Empress Cunigunde</a>, who was canonized in 1200 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Innocent_III" title="Pope Innocent III">Pope Innocent III</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> As the union produced no children, the German nobles elected <a href="/wiki/Conrad_II,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Conrad II, Holy Roman Emperor">Conrad II</a>, a great-great-grandson of <a href="/wiki/Emperor_Otto_I" class="mw-redirect" title="Emperor Otto I">Emperor Otto I</a>, to succeed him after his death in 1024. Conrad was the first of the <a href="/wiki/Salian_dynasty" title="Salian dynasty">Salian dynasty</a> of emperors.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life_and_marriage">Early life and marriage</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Henry_II,_Holy_Roman_Emperor&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life and marriage">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Henry was born in May 973,<sup id="cite_ref-Kampers_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kampers-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> the son of Duke <a href="/wiki/Henry_II_of_Bavaria" class="mw-redirect" title="Henry II of Bavaria">Henry II of Bavaria</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Gisela_of_Burgundy" title="Gisela of Burgundy">Gisela of Burgundy</a>. Through his father, he was the grandson of Duke <a href="/wiki/Henry_I_of_Bavaria" class="mw-redirect" title="Henry I of Bavaria">Henry I of Bavaria</a>, and the great-grandson of King <a href="/wiki/Henry_I_of_Germany" class="mw-redirect" title="Henry I of Germany">Henry I of Germany</a>. By his mother, he was the grandson of King <a href="/wiki/Conrad_I_of_Burgundy" title="Conrad I of Burgundy">Conrad I of Burgundy</a>, and the great-grandson of King <a href="/wiki/Rudolf_II_of_Burgundy" class="mw-redirect" title="Rudolf II of Burgundy">Rudolf II of Burgundy</a>.
</p><p>The elder Henry came into conflict with his cousin <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Emperor_Otto_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy Roman Emperor Otto II">Holy Roman Emperor Otto II</a>, in 974. The elder Henry and Otto II disputed each other's claims to authority over the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Swabia" title="Duchy of Swabia">Duchy of Swabia</a>: Henry claimed the duchy as his birthright while Otto II maintained his right to name a duke of his choosing. After an initial failed revolt, Otto II imprisoned the elder Henry in <a href="/wiki/Ingelheim" class="mw-redirect" title="Ingelheim">Ingelheim</a>. After escaping, Henry again <a href="/wiki/War_of_the_Three_Henries_(977-978)" class="mw-redirect" title="War of the Three Henries (977-978)">revolted against Otto II</a>. When this second revolt failed, Otto II deposed Henry as Duke of Bavaria and sent him into exile under the custody of the <a href="/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Utrecht_(695%E2%80%931580)" class="mw-redirect" title="Archdiocese of Utrecht (695–1580)">Bishop of Utrecht</a> in April 978. As a consequence of his revolt, the Emperor stripped the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Bavaria" title="Duchy of Bavaria">Duchy of Bavaria</a> of its southeastern territories bordering Italy and formed the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Carinthia" title="Duchy of Carinthia">Duchy of Carinthia</a>.
</p><p>During his father's exile, the younger Henry lived in <a href="/wiki/Hildesheim" title="Hildesheim">Hildesheim</a>. As a child he was educated in the Christian faith by Bishop <a href="/wiki/Wolfgang_of_Regensburg" title="Wolfgang of Regensburg">Wolfgang of Regensburg</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-SHMI_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SHMI-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> and then studied at the <a href="/wiki/Hildesheim_Cathedral" title="Hildesheim Cathedral">Hildesheim Cathedral</a>. The Emperor himself ensured the younger Henry received an ecclesiastical education in order that by becoming a religious official he would be prevented from participating in the Imperial government.
</p><p>The death of Otto II in 983 allowed the elder Henry to be released from custody and to return from exile. The elder Henry claimed <a href="/wiki/Regency" class="mw-redirect" title="Regency">regency</a> over <a href="/wiki/Otto_III" class="mw-redirect" title="Otto III">Otto III</a>, the three-year-old child of Otto II. After a failed attempt to claim the German throne for himself in 985, the elder Henry relinquished the regency to the child's mother <a href="/wiki/Theophanu" title="Theophanu">Theophanu</a>. In return for his submission to the child king, Henry was restored as <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Bavaria" class="mw-redirect" title="Duke of Bavaria">Duke of Bavaria</a>. The younger Henry, now thirteen years old, was named his regent over Bavaria. When the elder Henry died in 995, the younger Henry was elected by the Bavarian nobles as the new duke to succeed his father.
</p><p>In 999 Henry married <a href="/wiki/Cunigunde_of_Luxembourg" title="Cunigunde of Luxembourg">Cunigunde of Luxembourg</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> a daughter of Count <a href="/wiki/Siegfried_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Siegfried of Luxembourg">Siegfried of Luxembourg</a>. This marriage granted him an extensive network of contacts in Germany's western territories.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Reign_as_king">Reign as king</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Henry_II,_Holy_Roman_Emperor&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Reign as king">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Disputed_succession">Disputed succession</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Henry_II,_Holy_Roman_Emperor&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Disputed succession">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1002_German_royal_election" title="1002 German royal election">1002 German royal election</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:137px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Bas-c%C3%B4t%C3%A9_nord,_baie_VII_Henricus_Imperator_Babinbergensis_(dernier_tiers_XIIe).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Bas-c%C3%B4t%C3%A9_nord%2C_baie_VII_Henricus_Imperator_Babinbergensis_%28dernier_tiers_XIIe%29.jpg/135px-Bas-c%C3%B4t%C3%A9_nord%2C_baie_VII_Henricus_Imperator_Babinbergensis_%28dernier_tiers_XIIe%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="135" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Bas-c%C3%B4t%C3%A9_nord%2C_baie_VII_Henricus_Imperator_Babinbergensis_%28dernier_tiers_XIIe%29.jpg/203px-Bas-c%C3%B4t%C3%A9_nord%2C_baie_VII_Henricus_Imperator_Babinbergensis_%28dernier_tiers_XIIe%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Bas-c%C3%B4t%C3%A9_nord%2C_baie_VII_Henricus_Imperator_Babinbergensis_%28dernier_tiers_XIIe%29.jpg/270px-Bas-c%C3%B4t%C3%A9_nord%2C_baie_VII_Henricus_Imperator_Babinbergensis_%28dernier_tiers_XIIe%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="415" data-file-height="900" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Bas-c%C3%B4t%C3%A9_nord,_baie_VII_Henricus_Imperator_Babinbergensis_(dernier_tiers_XIIe).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>12th-century stained glass depiction of Henry II, <a href="/wiki/Strasbourg_Cathedral" title="Strasbourg Cathedral">Strasbourg Cathedral</a></div></div></div>
<p>In 1001, <a href="/wiki/Emperor_Otto_III" class="mw-redirect" title="Emperor Otto III">Emperor Otto III</a> experienced a revolt against his reign in <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a>. The Emperor sent word for Henry II to join him with reinforcements from <a href="/wiki/Germany" title="Germany">Germany</a>, but then died unexpectedly in January 1002. Otto was only 21 at the time of his death and had left no children and no instructions for the Imperial succession. In the <a href="/wiki/Ottonian_dynasty" title="Ottonian dynasty">Ottonian dynasty</a>, succession to the throne had belonged to the Saxon branch, not the Bavarian line of which Henry was a member. Rival candidates for the throne, including Count <a href="/wiki/Ezzo,_Count_Palatine_of_Lotharingia" title="Ezzo, Count Palatine of Lotharingia">Ezzo of Lotharingia</a>, Margrave <a href="/wiki/Eckard_I_of_Meissen" class="mw-redirect" title="Eckard I of Meissen">Eckard I of Meissen</a>, and Duke <a href="/wiki/Herman_II_of_Swabia" class="mw-redirect" title="Herman II of Swabia">Herman II of Swabia</a>, strongly contested Henry's right to succeed Otto III.
</p><p>As the <a href="/wiki/Funeral_procession" title="Funeral procession">funeral procession</a> moved through the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Bavaria" title="Duchy of Bavaria">Duchy of Bavaria</a> in February 1002, Henry met the procession in <a href="/wiki/Polling,_Weilheim-Schongau" title="Polling, Weilheim-Schongau">Polling</a>, just north of the Alps. To legitimize his claims, Henry demanded Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Heribert_of_Cologne" title="Heribert of Cologne">Heribert of Cologne</a> give him the <a href="/wiki/Imperial_Regalia" title="Imperial Regalia">Imperial Regalia</a>, chief among them being the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Lance" title="Holy Lance">Holy Lance</a>. Heribert, however, had sent these ahead of the procession, possibly out of distrust of Henry and possibly because he favored the succession of his relative Duke Herman II of Swabia as the next king. In order to force Herman II to relinquish the Holy Lance to him, Henry imprisoned the Archbishop and his brother the <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_W%C3%BCrzburg" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Würzburg">Bishop of Würzburg</a>. With neither the symbols of imperial authority, the crown jewels, nor the cooperation of Heribert, Henry was unable to convince the nobles attending Otto III's funeral procession to elect him as king. A few weeks later, at Otto III's funeral in <a href="/wiki/Aachen_Cathedral" title="Aachen Cathedral">Aachen Cathedral</a>, Henry again attempted to gain the support of the kingdom's nobles and was again rejected.
</p><p>So it was without the support of the kingdom's nobility that Henry took the radical action of having himself anointed and crowned <a href="/wiki/King_of_Germany" class="mw-redirect" title="King of Germany">King of Germany</a> ("Rex Romanorum") by <a href="/wiki/Willigis" title="Willigis">Willigis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Mainz" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Mainz">Archbishop of Mainz</a> on 9 July 1002 at <a href="/wiki/Mainz" title="Mainz">Mainz</a>, in present-day <a href="/wiki/Germany" title="Germany">Germany</a>. Henry's action marked the first time a German king was not crowned in <a href="/wiki/Aachen_Cathedral" title="Aachen Cathedral">Aachen Cathedral</a> since <a href="/wiki/Emperor_Otto_I" class="mw-redirect" title="Emperor Otto I">Emperor Otto I</a> began the tradition in 936 and the first time a German king assumed the throne without election by the German nobility. Under the regal name of "King Henry II", he appeared before the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Saxony" title="Duchy of Saxony">Saxons</a> in mid-July in full regal apparel. There, Henry convinced <a href="/wiki/Bernard_I,_Duke_of_Saxony" title="Bernard I, Duke of Saxony">Bernard I, Duke of Saxony</a>, to support his claims to the throne. In return for his support, Henry guaranteed Bernard's right to rule the Saxons and to represent their interests before him.
</p><p>Shortly after gaining the support of the Saxons, Henry arranged for Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Willigis" title="Willigis">Willigis</a> to crown his wife, <a href="/wiki/Cunigunde_of_Luxembourg" title="Cunigunde of Luxembourg">Cunigunde of Luxembourg</a> as Queen of Germany on 10 August 1002 in <a href="/wiki/Paderborn" title="Paderborn">Paderborn</a>, in present-day <a href="/wiki/Germany" title="Germany">Germany</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Consolidation_of_power">Consolidation of power</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Henry_II,_Holy_Roman_Emperor&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Consolidation of power">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Henry II spent the next several years consolidating his political power within his borders. <a href="/wiki/Herman_II,_Duke_of_Swabia" title="Herman II, Duke of Swabia">Herman II, Duke of Swabia</a>, in particular fiercely contested Henry II's right to the throne. The Swabian Duke believed he was Otto III's true successor, as he had married a daughter of <a href="/wiki/Liudolf,_Duke_of_Swabia" title="Liudolf, Duke of Swabia">Liudof</a>, eldest son of <a href="/wiki/Otto_I,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" class="mw-redirect" title="Otto I, Holy Roman Emperor">Emperor Otto I</a>. Armed conflicts between Henry II and Herman II broke out but proved to be inconclusive. This forced the two men to fight each other politically for the support of the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Swabia" title="Duchy of Swabia">Swabian</a> nobles.
</p><p>Unable to decisively defeat Herman in Swabia, Henry II attempted to legitimize his seizing the throne by traveling throughout the various duchies of his kingdom – <a href="/wiki/Saxony" title="Saxony">Saxony</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bavaria" title="Bavaria">Bavaria</a>, <a href="/wiki/Swabia" title="Swabia">Swabia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Upper_Lorraine" class="mw-redirect" title="Upper Lorraine">Upper Lorraine</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lower_Lorraine" class="mw-redirect" title="Lower Lorraine">Lower Lorraine</a>, <a href="/wiki/Franconia" title="Franconia">Franconia</a>. This was done in order to obtain the general consent of his subjects as opposed to traditional election. Henry II's familial ties to the <a href="/wiki/Ottonian_dynasty" title="Ottonian dynasty">Ottonian dynasty</a> eventually caused the kingdom's nobles to accept him as king. After being defeated at a battle near <a href="/wiki/Stra%C3%9Fburg,_Austria" title="Straßburg, Austria">Strasburg</a>, Herman II submitted to Henry II's authority on 1 October 1002. In exchange for this surrender, Henry II allowed Herman II to remain Duke of Swabia until his death the following year, after which, although recognizing the minor <a href="/wiki/Hermann_III,_Duke_of_Swabia" class="mw-redirect" title="Hermann III, Duke of Swabia">Hermann III</a> as his father's titular successor, Henry II effectively assumed all power over the Duchy himself.
</p><p>In 1003 <a href="/wiki/Henry_of_Schweinfurt" title="Henry of Schweinfurt">Henry of Schweinfurt</a>, <a href="/wiki/March_of_the_Nordgau" class="mw-redirect" title="March of the Nordgau">Margrave of Nordgau</a> in <a href="/wiki/Bavaria" title="Bavaria">Bavaria</a>, revolted against Henry II's rule. Henry II had promised to install the Margrave as his successor to the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Bavaria" title="Duchy of Bavaria">Duchy of Bavaria</a> in exchange for supporting his claim to the German crown. Upon assuming the throne, however, Henry II refused to honour his promise and instead supported the rights of the Bavarians to elect their own duke. With Henry II's support, <a href="/wiki/Counts_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Counts of Luxembourg">Count</a> <a href="/wiki/Henry_V,_Duke_of_Bavaria" title="Henry V, Duke of Bavaria">Henry I of Luxembourg</a> became the <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Bavaria" class="mw-redirect" title="Duke of Bavaria">Duke of Bavaria</a> as Henry V. Betrayed by the King, Margrave Henry allied with Bolesław I of Poland against him. However, his rebellion was soon quashed and the Nordgauian Margrave was deposed in 1004. Henry II then abolished the March of Nordgau, established the <a href="/wiki/Diocese_of_Bamberg" class="mw-redirect" title="Diocese of Bamberg">Diocese of Bamberg</a> in 1007, and transferred secular authority over the March's former territory to the Diocese in order to prevent further uprisings.<sup id="cite_ref-Borgolte2014_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Borgolte2014-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="First_Italian_expedition">First Italian expedition</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Henry_II,_Holy_Roman_Emperor&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: First Italian expedition">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The death of Otto III in 1002 and the resulting political turmoil over his successor allowed <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy_(medieval)" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Italy (medieval)">Italy</a> to fall from German control. <a href="/wiki/Margrave_of_Ivrea" class="mw-redirect" title="Margrave of Ivrea">Margrave</a> <a href="/wiki/Arduin_of_Ivrea" title="Arduin of Ivrea">Arduin of Ivrea</a> proclaimed himself <a href="/wiki/King_of_Italy" title="King of Italy">King of Italy</a> at <a href="/wiki/Pavia" title="Pavia">Pavia</a> soon after the Emperor's death.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Accompanied by <a href="/wiki/Arnulf_II,_Archbishop_of_Milan" class="mw-redirect" title="Arnulf II, Archbishop of Milan">Archbishop Arnulf II of Milan</a>, Arduin won the support of the Italian territorial magnates. Arduin, however, had been <a href="/wiki/Excommunicated" class="mw-redirect" title="Excommunicated">excommunicated</a> in 997 for the murder of the <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Vercelli" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Vercelli">Bishop of Vercelli</a>. This allowed Arduin's enemies in the Church, led by <a href="/wiki/Frederick,_Archbishop_of_Ravenna" class="mw-redirect" title="Frederick, Archbishop of Ravenna">Archbishop Frederick of Ravenna</a>, to side with the German King Henry II as the rightful ruler of <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a>. Henry II sent <a href="/wiki/Otto_I,_Duke_of_Carinthia" title="Otto I, Duke of Carinthia">Duke Otto I of Carinthia</a>, over the <a href="/wiki/March_of_Verona" title="March of Verona">March of Verona</a> to face Arduin, but Arduin successfully defeated Otto's troops at the Battle of Fabrica in 1003.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1004 Henry II responded to calls for aid from Italian bishops and led an invasion into <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a> against Arduin. Henry II gathered his troops at <a href="/wiki/Augsburg" title="Augsburg">Augsburg</a> and marched through the <a href="/wiki/Brenner_Pass" title="Brenner Pass">Brenner Pass</a> to <a href="/wiki/Trento" title="Trento">Trento</a>, Italy. After initial military successes, much of the Italian clergy and some noble families swore allegiance to Henry II, including <a href="/wiki/Arnulf_II,_Archbishop_of_Milan" class="mw-redirect" title="Arnulf II, Archbishop of Milan">Archbishop Arnulf II</a>. Joining Henry II in <a href="/wiki/Bergamo" title="Bergamo">Bergamo</a>, Arnulf II crowned him as <a href="/wiki/King_of_Italy" title="King of Italy">King of Italy</a> ("Rex Italiae") on 14 May 1004 in <a href="/wiki/Pavia" title="Pavia">Pavia</a>, in the <a href="/wiki/San_Michele_Maggiore,_Pavia" title="San Michele Maggiore, Pavia">Basilica of San Michele Maggiore</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Kampers_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kampers-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Unlike his predecessors, after gaining the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy" title="Kingdom of Italy">Kingdom of Italy</a> Henry II wore two crowns, one for Germany and one for Italy, instead of a common crown representing both realms.
</p><p>After the coronation a dispute arose between some of the residents and Henry's people. It escalated to where the residents attacked the palace where the king was dining. The army encamped outside the city swiftly moved to protect the king, and in the onslaught the city caught fire and many residents were killed.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After receiving the homage of the remaining Italian nobles, Henry returned to Germany in the early summer of 1004 without first traveling to <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a> to claim the Imperial crown. This is most likely due to opposition from <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XVIII" title="Pope John XVIII">Pope John XVIII</a>. Henry would not return to Italy for a decade, leaving the Kingdom to govern itself. Henry returned to Germany to take military action against the rebellious Boleslaw I of Poland.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Conflict_with_Poland">Conflict with Poland</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Henry_II,_Holy_Roman_Emperor&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Conflict with Poland">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/German-Polish_War_(1002%E2%80%931018)" class="mw-redirect" title="German-Polish War (1002–1018)">German-Polish War (1002–1018)</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Polish_relations">Polish relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Henry_II,_Holy_Roman_Emperor&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Polish relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti">.mw-parser-output .tmulti .multiimageinner{display:flex;flex-direction:column}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .theader{clear:both;font-weight:bold;text-align:center;align-self:center;background-color:transparent;width:100%}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbcaption{background-color:transparent}.mw-parser-output .thumbcaption{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow>.thumbcaption{text-align:center}}</style><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:268px;max-width:268px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:122px;max-width:122px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Henry2a.jpg" class="image"><img alt=""
